Trade pieces when ahead in material; avoid trades when behind and seek activity.\n6. Rooks to open files; knights to outposts; bishops on long diagonals.\n7. In endgames activate the king and push passed pawns.\n8. Verify every move is legal before playing; if refused, re-check the board.\n9. Resign only when truly lost; otherwise fight for a draw.\n10. }
```

}

I saved the brain as version 2 (144 words). What changed:
- **Rule 1 is stricter:** before every move I check every enemy piece that attacks the square I'm moving to, especially bishops and queens on long diagonals.

- **New tactics note:** look for pawn forks (my ...e4, attacking queen and rook, won the exchange) and checks that gain time.
- **Rule 5 is simpler:** when ahead, trade pieces and keep it simple.

The opening, development and endgame rules are kept, a little shorter.

> 3. Openings: White 1.e4 Italian (c3,d3,O-O,Re1 worked); Black e5 vs e4, d5 vs d4. Castle early.
> 4. Before every move, scan for free captures: undefended enemy pieces on lines my Q/R already sees (won Rb8 and Qh4 this way).
> 5. When ahead: offer queen trades, keep it simple. When behind: avoid trades, seek activity.
> 6. Watch for discovered checks against my king, and for back-rank mates; make luft when safe.
> 7. Games stop at move 40 and are scored on material; a 2-pawn edge was a draw. Build a big lead.
> 8. Near move 40, grab safe material rather than speculative mate tries.

```

-> Rd1 played. Now it is your opponent's move; end your turn.
